# Reconciled candidate screens

This is the candidate disposition record linked by the 30 authoritative rows in
[schemas.md](../schemas.md). It consumes the committed
[catalogue report](catalogue-portals.md) and [code-platform report](code-platforms.md);
no search was repeated. Each exact artifact appears once here, with all its keys.
Different imports are separate artifacts unless the source report proves equality;
a different version does not inherit another version's Fern verdict.

Screens run left to right: redistribution under [the licence rule](../../corpus-licensing.md),
immutable publisher provenance, Fern acceptance, then retention. Later screens say
not reached when an earlier screen blocks. An aggregation grant does not prove that
an artifact is the publisher's description. The evidence column retains the exact
blocker and the upstream trace, including uncertainty. An unfinished check is
`search-incomplete`, never `fern-rejected`; in particular, Attentive's stopped
78-minute run supplied no verdict. A completed generation that loses the purchased
shape is `witness-blocked` at retention, not absence.

These are candidate dispositions, not competing row outcomes. All 30 search outcomes
remain `search-incomplete` because the required source answers are incomplete.
The [previously rejected specs](../../../tests/fixtures/AGENTS.md#specs-already-tried-and-rejected-do-not-re-attempt-without-a-fix-upstream)
and [CORPUS screening decisions](../../../tests/fixtures/CORPUS.md) remain
canonical for their exact documents. In particular the Asana and Box catalogue
artifacts retain their recorded 17- and 25-diagnostic refusals. Their unproven
jentic transformations do not receive a second verdict on the catalogue bytes.

plus that path. Catalogue versions use the version references recorded in Appendix A.
